## Compact future-order auxiliary objective — 2026-10-19

The [2026 token-order paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2508.19228v2) motivates ranking first future token occurrences as additional language supervision. Its340Mgeneral results are mixed, and its coding comparisons use1.8B/7Bmodels and sampled pass@16/32/64. They do not establish sub1Bgreedy coding gains. The earlier decision to defer ordinary multi-token prediction remains: its original small-model results did not justify assuming the larger-model headline transfers here.

Our new variant shares the existing frozen vocabulary basis and adds a rank32residual transformation plus RMSNorm, rather than a full additional vocabulary matrix. At the actual1536hidden width, the head would add **99,840values** for an expected **999,806,282 total unique values** including the native, policy adapters and separate critic. No second vocabulary table/encoder, external weights or neural teacher is introduced. Actual full-size construction remains required. Ordinary text/image/cache inference continues through the original policy; the auxiliary head is only used by the explicit training objective.

All complete original next-token response/EOS targets retain supervision. The additional window16ranking uses each token's first future occurrence, including a repeat of the current input token, stops at the original response boundary, and excludes prompt/padding targets. The head uses the full-vocabulary normalizer, sparse gold targets, bounded32-position chunks and recomputation in backward. It adds computation; equal-FLOP or throughput gains are not claimed. This compact shared-basis design is a new research hypothesis, not a reproduction of the paper or a verified capability improvement.

The CPU fixture's 2.42 GB constructor source map also differs from the production episode's single 1.5 MB manifest; its hashing cost must not be presented as measured production overhead.

All 49,730,095 logical bytes, including repeated original bindings and the header, are counted despite a 720,439-byte compressed stream. Small-model research check — 2026-10-19

The publisher's [SmolLM2-360M model card](https://huggingface.co/HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-360M) reports four trillion pretraining tokens and 128 H100 GPUs. Our completed 1.106B target presentations include replay and are not an equivalent corpus or compute measure. This comparison supports investigating the foundation-data/compute gap; it does not establish a four-trillion-token minimum for this project or predict its eventual capability. No outside model weights were loaded.

The [Tiny Recursive Model paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04871) reports a seven-million-parameter recursive network evaluated on ARC tasks. Those task-specific results do not establish general text, coding or multimodal parity with a high-end LLM. Shared recursive computation remains a candidate for a controlled later ablation, with the complete own-model baseline, code correctness/efficiency and broad retention assessments preserved. It is not a demonstrated solution to our current capability gap.

The earlier failed small test harness is preserved, and the corrected four-worker test passes. Additional52,970,300OS/network/security/crypto code tokens, the49klater instruction sources, broad original source coverage, useful multimodal learning, genuine-human policy training, full independent benchmarks and a qualified final export remain required. **All ten final-model requirements remain incomplete; no frontier parity or candidate promotion is claimed.**

See [live warm-up](reports/verified-views-execution-v1/warmup-progress.json), [actual fresh GPU capacity](reports/verified-views-execution-v1/capacity.json), [source views](reports/verified-code-views-v1/independent-reader-v1.json), [causal rewards](reports/causal-code-rewards-v2/independent-reader.json), [prior actual outcomes](reports/protected-joint-development2048-readback-v1.json) and [full-scope review](reports/verified-views-execution-v1-review.json). Dated entries below are historical.

Protected joint training, **2026-10-17**: the own **984,399,690-parameter** student is now running a new four-L40 objective, with **125 actual production updates** observed. Image/word batches update vision and reading through a fixed language decoder. Text batches use a **5e-6 peak language learning rate** and **0.1 full-context KL** to a frozen copy of our own initial model. The additional **984,399,690 reference values are training-only**, so resident policy plus reference is **1,968,799,380**; the reference is excluded from the exported under1B student.

The preceding full-weight recipe was retired for persistent coding regression. These are matched **development** results, with code budget128 and math256, separate from full512-token endpoints:

| Measure | Initial | 1,024 updates | 2,048 updates |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Code syntax | 20/24 | 9/24 | 12/24 |
| Code functional correctness | 1/24 | 0/24 | 0/24 |
| Code efficiency | 0/24 | 0/24 | 0/24 |
| Generated knowledge | 0/56 | 0/56 | 1/56 |
| Math exact answers | 1/16 | 0/16 | 0/16 |
| OCR exact | 0/20 | 0/20 | 0/20 |
| OCR character error | 0.954 | 2.015 | 0.979 |

All **72 code programs were independently reexecuted** and raw knowledge/math/OCR outcomes reconciled. The saved2,048-update state contains **445 actual Adam states / 14,286,535 targets**, with every selected language/vision/word tensor changed. The original model and evaluated checkpoints remain preserved; later uncheckpointed updates are not claimed as retained weights. The automatic all-front nonimprovement rule correctly took no action after one extra knowledge answer; the separate manual handover explicitly prioritizes the still-regressing coding milestone. This is an incomplete source epoch and incomplete full endpoint comparison, not a promoted result.

The protected replacement passed fresh configured **all-four-L40 full-length book/word/image capacity and exact weight/Adam/all-worker RNG recovery**. Each worker retains **445 Adam states**, proves nonzero own-reference KL, keeps language bytes exact during image updates, and keeps the reference unchanged. Peak allocated is about **27.11 GiB**, reserved about **30.06 GiB** per worker. Its short full-size CPU recovery/cold-load test and actual positive-KL backward also pass. **No protected-model held gain is established yet.**

The broken leading-row object subset and missing native vision-view property are preserved as failures and corrected in new evaluation versions. Matched CPU object scores6/30to7/30remain no better than blank/wrong7/30; CPU caption retrieval I2T1/32to1/32 and T2I3/32to1/32 remain weak. The new driver uses validated object controls and the exact own vision module. Its complete source curriculum is still **458,450 source-family records / 156,677,748 targets /22,065planned updates**. Additional admitted OS/network/security code, complete independent endpoints and held word checks, controlled production execution RL and genuine-human policy learning remain required. All ten final-model requirements remain incomplete.

See [live protected progress](reports/protected-joint-v1/progress.json), [fresh four-GPU proof](reports/protected-joint-v1/capacity.json), [actual earlier state readback](reports/fast-joint-step2048-readback-v1/complete.json), [raw development outcome readback](reports/fast-joint-development2048-readback-v1.json) and [current full-scope review](reports/protected-joint-production-v1-review.json). The dated entries below are historical. **No high-end parity or final candidate promotion is claimed.**

Current joint learning, **2026-10-17**: the own **984,399,690-parameter** student is now training on **all four local L40s**, with **400 actual production updates** observed. It updates **931,244,544 language**, **51,624,448 vision** and **407,618 word-head** values. The selected full epoch contains **458,450 source-family records / 156,677,748 target tokens** across code, education, dialogue, math, generic photos and text-bearing images; **22,065 updates** are planned. These are selected source-family exposures, not certified distinct new raw examples or exhaustive public-code coverage.

Actual configured four-GPU full-batch training and exact weight/Adam/CPU/private-CUDA RNG recovery passed. A source/schedule reader reconciles the first **78 production updates**, covering **1,632 complete source-family records / 586,124 targets**, and measured mean GPU busy percentages **[99.6, 100.0, 91.6, 95.2]** over five one-second samples. This is activity/source evidence, not a new held quality score or useful-FLOPs utilization. The earlier zero-update wrapper stalled during initial evaluation; its exact workers were stopped and restored before the new controller acquired the GPUs. Development checks now run in isolated workers with a 180-second budget, after learning begins. Full native endpoint evaluations retain their original 512-token code/math protocols.

The custom execution-RL/public-target fallback also passes an actual **999,706,442-parameter CPU mechanics proof**: two disposable logical updates, exact next sampled actions/rewards/weights/Adam/RNG recovery, and trained native text/image/word cold reload. It uses only **four actions per sample**; this is not full-horizon GPU capacity, production RL, genuine human feedback or a measured coding gain. When every sample fails its published train examples, the objective explicitly adds the complete verified public solution. All ten final-model requirements remain incomplete; the latest local functional/efficiency score is still **0/24**. The full human-feedback stage and the separately admitted **52,970,300 new OS/network/security code tokens** remain required.

The old unstarted word/foundation/preference/PPO controllers were intentionally retired for this faster joint run; the dated queue statements below are historical. Sources, failed/partial reports and completed older training remain preserved. See [live plan](reports/fast-joint-v3/plan.json), [live progress](reports/fast-joint-v3/progress.json), [source/schedule readback](reports/fast-joint-v3-progress-readback-v1.json), [full-size hybrid mechanics](reports/execution-group-RL-full-CPU-v1/complete.json), and [current full-scope review](reports/fast-joint-v3-and-execution-hybrid-v1-review.json). **No candidate is promoted and no high-end parity is claimed.**

The fixed requirements are an original model trained from scratch, public data, fewer than one billion total deployed parameters, text and image inputs, and human-feedback training. The desired high-end LLM comparison is a research target, not a capability claim. Architecture design alone cannot verify it; sustained full-size training and independent benchmarks remain required.

The current 706M dense baseline was chosen to make text/vision training, causality, checkpointing, and human-feedback objectives inspectable before introducing more experimental mechanisms. It uses standard architectural building blocks implemented here rather than weights from an existing model. Establishing a stable learning baseline is necessary to distinguish improvements from pipeline faults.

[Training Compute-Optimal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.15556) motivates balancing parameter count and training data. Its approximate dense training compute relation, `6ND`, is used in the budget calculator. This approximation is not a vision/RL simulator and does not predict benchmark performance. The appropriate training-token budget for a fixed small deployment model can be much larger than a training-compute-optimal budget.

The official [SmolLM2-360M model card](https://huggingface.co/HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-360M) reports four trillion training tokens. This is a useful reality check on the data scale behind capable small language models. A 20B-token pilot can test our recipe and efficiency; it should not be treated as equivalent to a mature small-model training program.

[Learning Transferable Visual Models From Natural Language Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.00020) supports learning image representations from image/text supervision at scale. It does not imply that a randomly initialized image encoder learns general visual understanding from a few VQA examples. Our image-prefix model currently has an executable caption/VQA SFT path; image/caption pretraining at scale and contrastive warmup are pending experiments.

[Training Language Models to Follow Instructions With Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.02155) provides the demonstration, learned reward, and reinforcement-learning pattern. We implemented reward modeling plus PPO with a critic, a fixed reference policy, sampled KL penalties, GAE, and clipped policy/value updates. Completing these steps on a tiny replica proves implementation paths run; it does not prove that our reward model generalizes or that PPO improves helpfulness.

[Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.18290) provides a simpler alternative to an explicit reward-model/RL pipeline. Our DPO implementation uses response-only sequence log-probabilities and compares policy log-ratios against a fixed reference. Human and AI feedback provenance is recorded separately. Multimodal human preferences still need collecting or importing; the current public preference sample is text only.

[Phi-4-Mini-Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21233) motivates combining distilled reasoning data, supervised training, preferences, and verifiable-reward RL. [Small Models Struggle to Learn From Strong Reasoners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12143) cautions that long teacher solutions can exceed a small student's learnability. A proposed experiment is to compare verified short solutions and a difficulty curriculum against indiscriminate long-chain distillation, using public released traces. No teacher model has been called, and no teacher-generated private dataset is being presumed available.

[Better & Faster Large Language Models via Multi-token Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19737) motivates future-token auxiliary losses as a possible sample-efficiency intervention. Our current code implements next-token prediction only. A proposed equal-compute ablation will compare one, two, and four prediction targets; any extra head parameters and training FLOPs must be included in the size and budget accounting.

[Less Is More: Recursive Reasoning With Tiny Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04871) is an interesting example of small specialized reasoning systems. Its puzzle-specific results do not demonstrate that recursion converts a sub-billion multimodal language model into a general high-end LLM. Weight sharing and recurrent-depth experiments should be judged under equal total compute and with broad held-out tests, rather than parameter count alone.

The next research sequence is: use the verified four-L40 allocation; broaden and ablate the initial tokenizer; establish public language/code/math data mixtures; run a bounded text pilot with held-out loss and task benchmarks; warm up and train the random vision path on image/caption data before broader VQA; collect or import genuine multimodal preferences; evaluate the reward model before using it for PPO; then compare curriculum, auxiliary prediction, recurrence, and verifiable-reward techniques independently. Larger-scale training should follow measured improvement, not an assumed frontier-equivalence claim.

Required comparison evidence includes independently held-out language/reasoning/coding tasks, image-grounded questions that cannot be answered from text alone, blindness/image-shuffle ablations, preference-model ranking quality, human policy comparisons, latency and peak VRAM, and matched context/tool/test-time-compute budgets against the named high-end comparator. Current reports cover CPU-replica learning, four-L40 readiness, source/domain provenance, held-out loss and an eight-task Python generation baseline. Broad capability and comparator access are still missing.

Current full-size evidence: the nominal 706M configuration completed a short synthetic text-and-image CPU optimizer test and saved one-step public-text and public-VQA checkpoints. A new 32K tokenizer and a bounded document-separated packed corpus are prepared. These demonstrate executable initialization and training paths, not a useful language or vision model. Four-L40 CUDA BF16 and distributed runtime are now verified. A 238M-token original GitHub code/documentation collection plus domain-reference and public-domain book collection supports the first sustained mixture pilot. Sustained learning outcomes and high-end comparison remain unverified.


The code pilot will use one almost-complete pass through the expanded mixture, with code dominating its token share. It keeps the initial tokenizer and architecture fixed so a before/after held-out loss comparison is interpretable. Follow-up experiments should compare a code/domain tokenizer, longer training, function-completion SFT derived only from training files, and verifiable rewards from isolated compiler/test execution. Evaluation must extend beyond the eight elementary Python cases to independent problems across languages, repository-level edits, dependency/API use, and unseen test suites. Fewer held-out tokens or a declining pretraining loss does not prove useful code generation.

The first visual curriculum has been broadened from grayscale category images to [Open Images / Localized Narratives](https://google.github.io/localized-narratives/) human-described everyday scenes, [TextOCR](https://textvqa.org/textocr/dataset/) photographed text and word regions, actual GovInfo legislation page images, and licensed Commons scientific images. A small Fashion-MNIST auxiliary task does not represent general vision. Contrastive warmup trains our own random vision encoder against our own frozen text representations, followed by image-conditioned response training. Held-out image/caption retrieval, blank/mismatched-image interventions, and separate OCR transcription errors test whether the visual input is useful. They cannot establish broad reasoning or frontier-model parity. Text crops accommodate the initial 224px encoder; variable resolution, tiling, position handling and broader chart/document QA remain research work.

The encoder now accepts supported square resolutions from 224px to 448px by [interpolating its learned position grid](https://arxiv.org/html/2010.11929v2), with unchanged checkpoint tensor shapes and parameter count. Causal cached inference and position-grid gradients have been checked at the higher resolution. Full-model backward fits the measured L40 resource check at batch two/512 text tokens; full optimizer memory, longer contexts and actual OCR gains require separate measurements. The final joint pilot uses 448px and compares inference at both resolutions on identical held-out samples.

The initial text pilot's code likelihood improved substantially while executable algorithm generation remained 0/24. Forced-choice domain accuracy stayed near chance. Instruction-format code produced more syntactically valid outputs without correct algorithms. This distinguishes source-pattern learning, output formatting and task understanding. Greater representative pretraining and code-instruction coverage remain necessary; the current small visual/human-feedback pilots are not a substitute for that requirement. Their held-out results should guide later experiments without redefining success around the current architecture or test suite.

The next curriculum adds large dated Wikipedia article coverage and original six-language [CodeSearchNet](https://github.com/github/CodeSearchNet) documentation/implementation pairs. This directly targets the observed gap between source continuation and following a programming specification. Published examples can rely on their surrounding modules and source-era APIs, so corpus growth and static syntax filtering cannot substitute for executable held-out tests. Old canonical article/file splits and official held-out repositories are retained. Larger language training will change the language feature space, so subsequent own-encoder visual realignment and fresh human-feedback training are part of the queued sequence rather than assuming the earlier alignment remains valid.

The first full multimodal pilot is now evaluated: 448px OCR exact match is 1/170, image-to-caption retrieval is 2/128 (chance 1/128), executable code remains 0/24 in both formats, and forced-choice knowledge is 12.16% versus 11.18% chance. A controlled 32-example training/validation OCR probe at the image-reading and joint checkpoints produced zero exact matches in each case. Correct-image likelihood improves over blank images, but this is weak image dependence, not reliable transcription. The probes support investigating basic visual/character learning rather than treating the problem as established memorization followed by generalization failure.

[SynthTIGER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.09313) motivates an exact-label lettering supplement. Our independent local renderer uses public-domain book words and sampled characters with recorded public font notices; it does not import pretrained OCR weights, call a teacher, or claim to reproduce SynthTIGER. The experiment adds 50K training crops and 2K validation crops with held-out normalized text groups and font styles. It preserves real photographs, real OCR and text demonstrations during joint training, then compares real OCR, photo interventions, code and knowledge against the original DPO policy. Promotion requires measured reading improvement with regression guards; otherwise the longer pipeline retains the original policy. The evaluated validation sets are now tuning sets. Separate new-image, new-document and independently held-out final assessments are still required, and synthetic lettering success cannot establish broad visual capability.

The first reward branch exposed a numerical issue rather than only a learning failure: final scalar projections under BF16 produced 69 ties and 505/1000 correct rankings. Recomputing the scalar projection in FP32 from identical BF16 backbone activations produced 538/1000 correct rankings and zero ties. The normal evaluator reproduced this result after the model fix. The backbone, parameters, source pairs and pretrained-weight policy were unchanged. Critic projections remain FP32 in forward and cached inference; GPU reward/PPO resume checks require the same projection semantics. This narrowly clears the predefined offline gate (lower 95% bound 50.70%) and enables a bounded PPO experiment; it does not validate reward fidelity on new own-policy outputs. Original reports and the controlled scheduler transition are retained.

Captioning and OCR do not cover general image questions. The next visual stage adds original Open Images box-derived region identification, class-instance counting and 2D spatial questions. [The publisher's annotation definitions](https://storage.googleapis.com/openimages/web/factsfigures_v6.html) motivate excluding crowd boxes from exact counts and preserving class-specific exhaustive annotations rather than inferring negatives from absent labels. Boxes are rotated with their photos; targets must remain visible at the configured 448px resolution. Questions do not disclose the spatial answer through numeric coordinates. Blank/mismatched-image generation and likelihood controls test dependence on the photo. This introductory curriculum shares the earlier photo splits and is not an independently held-out broad VQA test.

The source-comment/code curriculum was previously unexecuted and can rely on its original environment. A separate program supplement now checks literal published behavior: 673 original programs and 2,692 name variants pass their source examples in the constrained Python worker. The original [MBPP source split](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-research/google-research/f46ca8374b4cddef97ca4208ad986049d74d296a/mbpp/README.md) supplies only IDs 601–974; validation/test references and exact local specifications are excluded. Source licences and transformations remain attached. A focused two-pass stage is queued after the larger six-language instruction stage, with language/instruction retention. Tests and name variants are small checks/exposures, not proofs of unseen-case correctness, efficiency, or universal code ability.

The broader public code assessment prepares 175 supported problems from the 257 sanitized MBPP test tasks. Eligibility is determined before model evaluation from static literal-test extraction and reference execution, with all 82 exclusions recorded. Exact specific-reference checks apply to the new verified training view; the earlier full pretraining corpora are not contamination-certified. Zero-shot instruction generation differs from the publisher's original few-shot setup. Report supported-subset scores explicitly, continue the private randomized/large-input checks, and add native languages, library/API and repository-level evaluations before making broad coding claims.

The first native assessment covers 12 ISO C++17 function contracts using a hash-verified official Zig toolchain. Linux filesystem/syscall restrictions are installed before candidate execution, including static initialization; compilation is also constrained. Six checks exercise confinement, setup failure, timeout and a deliberately quadratic implementation. All reference functions pass 1,724 hidden cases and large-input stress, while the trained DPO branch passes no task even at compilation. This establishes working evaluation machinery and a poor measured baseline. Parent-measured reference-relative runtime includes protocol transport, so it remains separate from Python/JavaScript timing and cannot certify asymptotic complexity. Native library/API use, additional languages and repository-level work remain outside this assessment.

The lettering intervention completed and failed its promotion rule: real OCR exact match remained zero and completion-code syntax regressed. The original DPO policy was retained. A fixed 1.254B-training-token mixture of completed Wikipedia parts and six-language code now supports actual continued foundation learning while more articles collect. The later full-corpus passes reuse these tokens; they are recorded as extra exposure. A committed-part snapshot is immutable and preserves both source checksums and original held-out assignments. This reduces idle training time without treating partially written collection files as completed data.

Original [GSM8K human solutions](https://github.com/openai/grade-school-math) add explicit multi-step arithmetic demonstrations. The new supplement reserves an internal validation split and protects all official test questions, excluding the author's generated Socratic/model-solution variants. Arithmetic annotation checks are bounded rational calculations, not execution of source code or proof of whole-solution validity. Greedy tool-free evaluation uses exact numeric final answers and reports bounded samples separately from full official test coverage. These are elementary word problems, so broader symbolic/advanced mathematics, physical reasoning and independent final assessments remain required.

Offline human preference optimization must also preserve measured capability. The original DPO branch improved reference-adjusted chosen/rejected likelihood on 540/1,000 held-out pairs, yet its paired local reports lost instruction-code syntax, caption metrics and OCR error. PPO improved the relative preference margin on only 471/1,000 pairs, with a negative mean margin. A retrospective partial diagnostic records these observations without pretending that its older missing provenance supports full policy selection. The next feedback experiment compares nine declared capabilities against its pre-feedback policy, includes language/subject/task checks, and retains the base when a branch fails. Source/runtime/tokenizer/pixel/budget matching reduces protocol confounds. This is a development selection rule; own-policy human judgements, reward-exploitation tests and fresh independent benchmarks remain required.

Synthetic word exact match also remained 0/256 after the lettering intervention. A frozen-character probe compares the same own-encoder architecture and renderer inputs across original DPO, lettering warmup, joint reading and random initialization. Its fixed ridge head uses a coarse 2x2 spatial feature grid, train-only standardization and 744 held-out-font glyphs covering 62 classes. Accuracy increased from 23.4% to 33.1% at warmup (random control 22.2%); joint reading scored 31.0%. The probe's training accuracy is only 55.2% at warmup, so the classifier/coarse representation can underfit. These conditional observations support investigating denser character and sequence supervision while avoiding a claim that the visual encoder is fully competent and only the language decoder is failing.

The next reading hypothesis is an auxiliary character-sequence objective on our own encoder. [Connectionist Temporal Classification](https://www.cs.toronto.edu/~graves/icml_2006.pdf) sums possible label alignments without requiring per-character segmentation; [scene-text sequence recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/1507.05717) supplies a relevant application. A proposed experiment can use our own language embedding rows as fixed character prototypes and train the existing vision/projector weights, preserving the deployed parameter count and avoiding pretrained OCR weights. The fixed-own-prototype experiment is implemented and completed one pass, but it did not improve exact word reading: 0/256 under greedy path collapse, with 252 empty outputs, and 0/128 in a bounded prefix-probability diagnostic. Prototype vectors are distinct (maximum off-diagonal cosine 0.313), so nearly identical prototypes do not explain the result. Lower loss did not demonstrate reading ability. It remains unpromoted; a different readout/supervision design requires further investigation. It must preserve licensed real photos/reading data and pass matched real-word/document/grounding/code/knowledge checks. Single-glyph diagnostics cannot replace those assessments or broader independent multimodal evaluation.

The less constrained follow-up learns height pooling and local temporal/character readout weights instead of fixing character directions to language embeddings. It uses the same own encoder and public transcript splits, adding only 0.878M parameters within the sub-billion budget. The head can memorize eight actual training words on frozen encoder features, establishing a working optimization path without establishing generalization. The full three-pass experiment warms the head before updating the encoder at a smaller rate. Its held-out results must distinguish sequence learning from blank/prior output and then test native multimodal behavior; a separate CTC head alone cannot establish the requested general model capability.

The interim learned-readout check now distinguishes actual training exposure from mere training-split membership. At update 1,100, only 24 of the 64 hash-selected training cases had been presented, and none was read exactly; none of 64 validation cases was read exactly either. Proper/wrong-image CTC likelihood differences indicate image dependence while decoding remains poor. These observations support waiting for the declared three-pass result before adapting this head into the native multimodal path. A finite lower loss or eight-word memorization sanity check cannot substitute for reading evidence.

A separate verified-program SFT intervention isolates whether checked, standalone public programs improve the own partially trained foundation policy before the larger curriculum reaches its coding stages. It uses two passes over 2,692 name variants of 673 source programs, fresh before/after Python/JavaScript completion and instruction checks, and fixed inference/implementation matching. Program renaming and repetitions do not expand algorithm diversity. Any local improvement remains a curriculum hypothesis; other-language, knowledge, image and preference preservation plus independent assessments remain necessary before selecting a general policy.

The additional AI2D source review found conflicting terms: the [AWS dataset listing](https://registry.opendata.aws/allenai-diagrams/) states CC BY-SA, while `ai2d/license.txt` inside the [publisher-linked archive](https://ai2-public-datasets.s3.amazonaws.com/diagrams/ai2d-all.zip) states a non-commercial research/evaluation grant with further restrictions. The exact inspected license, archive ETag/size and local license hash are retained in `reports/ai2d-source-review-v1.json` and `data/source-reviews/ai2d-v1/license.txt`. No examples from this additional archive were admitted to training, and the full 991MB archive was not downloaded. An updated grant would need verification before this supplement is prepared; existing photo/reading data and running jobs continue.

The verified-program intervention completed and showed a narrower gain than the target requires: completion syntax improved 8/24 to 15/24, instruction syntax regressed 14/24 to 9/24, and correctness/efficiency remained zero in both modes. A lower demonstration loss is not reliable algorithmic generalization. This supports retaining broader instruction and multilingual data in the main coding curriculum instead of promoting this code-only candidate.

The next generic-vision hypothesis uses [masked autoencoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.06377) on the same own vision backbone, without an external teacher. It processes only visible patches and predicts masked, per-patch-normalized RGB values with a small temporary decoder. Our RMSNorm/SwiGLU backbone, learned positions and bounded data/augmentation budget differ from the original paper; this is an adapted experiment, not a reproduction of its accuracy. The new full-image set adds the completed Commons collection and removes the earlier clothing-category task from this objective. Held-out full-image masks remain fixed across before/after assessments. Reconstruction improvement can occur in the decoder or through visual priors, so semantic-feature probes and matched native multimodal retention must establish any useful encoder gain before adoption.

The completed learned CTC readout produced only one image-specific exact synthetic word among 256 development transcripts; both correct real digits also appeared with mismatched images. Character-error reduction did not establish reliable real-word or native reading. That component remains unpromoted.

A frozen object-crop probe adds evidence beyond reconstruction and isolated glyphs, while explicitly supplying oracle boxes and fitting only diagnostic heads. Original own features scored 20.4% versus 23.5% for a matched random encoder on 221 crops/190 held-out photo groups; the paired interval includes zero. Low training accuracy and coarse fixed ridge features limit causal attribution. Geometry/color controls and frozen-weight hashes bound some shortcuts without establishing semantic/general capability. After masked pretraining finishes, an automatic matched comparison reuses the fixed cases and baseline protocol and resamples whole photo groups. It cannot substitute for caption alignment, native image interpretation, OCR, code/knowledge retention or broad independent final tests.

Masked pretraining finished and produced a tentative object-feature gain: 24.4% versus 20.4% original crop accuracy, with a paired interval spanning -0.92 to +8.92 percentage points; the random control scored 23.5%. Macro accuracy improved to 18.1%, but the same coarse supervised probe, oracle boxes and reused development cases limit the result. The generic reconstruction encoder remains separate from the native multimodal policy. Its lower pixel loss cannot certify image reasoning or reading.

The final author-published OpenAssistant conversation release supplies a larger reviewed, multilingual human instruction supplement. Source flags and human reviews are kept distinct from model training provenance; original texts, tree structure, official validation roots, exact reserved questions and own-tokenizer limits are checked. The resulting 11,985 demonstrations span 25 languages, although some languages have only a few retained examples. A new joint stage follows broad training with finalized additional real photos, reviewed dialogues and full code/math/image rehearsal, updating both vision and language before the existing nine-family capability and offline human-pair guards precede fresh feedback training. It targets instruction retention without claiming that volunteer ratings prove factual/algorithmic correctness or that offline preference improvement proves own-policy human quality.

A paired caption-alignment experiment now isolates the original versus masked-pretrained own vision encoder on identical 2,000-update foundation language tensors. Both use the same public full-image caption subset, contrastive/SFT budgets, inference protocols and held-out image interventions. Language and critic tensors are frozen and checked exactly after each stage; the masked decoder and diagnostic classifiers are excluded. The test asks whether reconstruction features help the native multimodal path after caption alignment. Caption labels alone do not teach reliable transcription or object-question formats, so OCR/QA remain separate retention evidence. No native gain has yet been established and neither branch is automatically promoted.

The seen-code readout separates token fitting from valid free generation: 98.5% teacher-forced token accuracy yielded only 12/32 published-example passes after SFT. Valid syntax on 30/32 responses alongside incorrect algorithms/returns supports testing a sequence-level execution-derived preference objective. The new twenty-pair pilot prefers original public implementations over the same policy's reproducibly failing responses. This is synthetic verifier feedback, not human preference, and correctness under a few visible examples is a limited reward signal. Fresh held-out coding checks and a separate broader retention decision are necessary; this pilot never changes production selection.

The short execution-preference trial changed known source-program correctness from twelve to fourteen of thirty-two, but seven gains were offset by five lost correct programs. Neither held-out Python format gained a correct or efficient solution, and completion syntax regressed. This rejects promotion and limits the evidence for a tiny static negative set: verifier preferences can shift local behavior without algorithmic generalization or retention. Broader foundation/instruction training, representative replay and fresh own-policy verification remain necessary; a few visible source examples do not provide a complete coding reward.

The next data-scale hypothesis adds a larger licensed, human-captioned ordinary-photo subset from the same original publisher release. Current native retrieval is weak and original-branch OCR/QA remain zero; eighteen thousand full images do not establish broad multimodal ability. The additional set protects existing parent/source/pixel splits and screens coarse perceptual aliases, with smaller JPEG storage to preserve room for training. JPEG pixels are explicitly versioned and the source originals remain reproducible by recorded download hashes/URLs. Data collection alone is not a capability gain; a future training/evaluation handoff must admit the finalized manifest without changing the active training inputs.

Matched caption alignment did not turn masked reconstruction into useful native vision: masked image-to-caption retrieval was zero versus four of 128 original, while both had zero exact OCR and object answers. Likelihood/error changes cannot justify transferring this encoder. A frozen caption-feature diagnostic found severe final-EOS directional concentration (mean cosine .989), while content pooling produced more directional variance. This is a plausible weakness in the frozen contrastive target, especially on the partially trained language snapshot, but vector diversity does not establish semantics. An explicit matched objective ablation would be needed before changing training or interpreting alternative retrieval measures as capability.

The next explicit ablation tests whether content-mean frozen caption features provide a better contrastive signal than the highly concentrated final-EOS features. It uses identical own initial language/vision tensors, public captions, training budget and supervised image-conditioned follow-up. Both branches are evaluated under the same new readout, with default EOS retrieval and native likelihood/OCR/QA kept separate. The experimental adapter is local to one process and does not modify the shared trainer or active curriculum. A representation/readout benefit without native image-use improvement remains insufficient for promotion.

The completed content-mean ablation did not improve native correctness. Its matching-readout retrieval was 2/128 image-to-text and 5/128 text-to-image, versus control 1/128 and 2/128; default EOS retrieval fell from 4/128 and 3/128 to 1/128 each. OCR remained 0/170 exact and objects 0/164, with worse OCR character error. This rejects promotion on the present evidence. The partially trained language snapshot and small reused development cohorts limit conclusions about objectives on a mature model. No shared trainer or production policy was changed.

An audit found the original TextOCR sampler used at most four word crops per training photo. A bounded supplement now retains 23,847 previously unused human word annotations from 2,143 existing admitted parents, with no new held-out parents or generated text. It stops at 2 GiB and preserves the original annotation/source-byte chain, including the explicit unverified-mirror limitation. The queued next curriculum uses a dedicated frozen-language real-word vision stage before joint additional photos, reviewed dialogue and all prior rehearsal. More words within the same scenes increase supervised exposures, not independent visual diversity; ordinary-photo collection addresses a different gap. Held-out native transcription and image controls remain required, and documents/charts/general visual reasoning remain unachieved.

The 4,785-update foundation warmup completed 1.254B target-token exposures. Matched 64-window validation NLL improved from 1.608 to 0.866 on code and 4.723 to 3.052 on Wikipedia. Generated functional/efficiency checks nevertheless remain 0/24 in both completion and instruction formats; completion syntax decreased from 9/24 to 3/24, and instruction syntax remained 0/24. The generated examples include mixed-language attributes, wrong indexing, repeated blocks and non-code responses. On all 559 reserved domain questions, generated accuracy fell from 3.94% to 2.68%, and letter-format compliance from 32.02% to 16.28%. Forced-choice likelihood accuracy increased from 12.34% to 13.42%, but its 95% interval [10.84%,16.49%] includes the 11.18% average chance baseline. All 112 vision tensors remained exact. This is evidence of token-distribution learning without useful specification-following, algorithmic generalization or reliable knowledge. The frozen next curriculum preserves the code-instruction/verified-program/math stages after the larger text pass; it cannot assume code or instruction competence from language loss alone. Evidence: `reports/foundation-warmup-assessment-v1/comparison.json`.

A nominal-model decoding diagnostic found that four failing short code trajectories agreed at every full/cache next-token decision under both FP32 and BF16. Full FP32 still mixed language conventions and repeated specification/comments. The small checked contexts do not identify a numerical cache defect causing these failures; they do not establish universal cache correctness. A matched intervention now varies foundation pretraining depth (1,000 versus 4,785 updates) while preserving the verified-program SFT data and two-pass budget. Fresh older held-out assessments and identical 32-program learning diagnostics will distinguish improved fitting from functional generalization. The main longer broad/code/instruction curriculum remains intact, and no experimental policy is automatically selected.

The mature verified-program SFT completed its matched 673-update budget with frozen vision preserved. Completion syntax increased from 3/24 to 16/24 and instruction syntax from 0/24 to 10/24, but all held-out functional/efficiency rates remain zero. Early outputs include valid but wrong indexing/formulas and empty-input errors, so improved grammatical structure is not algorithmic correctness. The distinct seen-example assessment and fresh older-SFT control below measure the remaining gap; pretraining-depth benefits cannot be inferred from syntax or declining training loss alone.

The finalized pretraining-depth comparison found improved seen-program fitting: the older/newer supervised branches pass 12/32 versus 20/32 original published-example checks, with teacher-forced accuracy 98.53% versus 99.39%, and the same 30/32 seen syntax passes. The newer unsupervised base passes zero. Fresh older/newer held-out functional/efficiency remains zero in each code mode, with syntax gains only 15→16/24 and 9→10/24. These reused seen examples are visible in prompts, so they measure fitting rather than independent generalization. The result supports investigating stronger task-grounded and diverse supervision, rather than treating more foundation tokens or high teacher-forced accuracy as sufficient evidence of useful code generation.

Original Exercism tracks provide a different supervised source: explicit problems, starter interfaces, full reference modules and published tests across six languages. The finalized 599-training/181-validation implementation corpus protects each exercise group across ports, screens reserved Python structural aliases and verifies 327 supported literal Python cases. Complete original targets are preserved; no external model teacher generates descriptions or code. Publisher authorship and unexecuted interfaces/languages remain unverified rather than being labelled universally correct. The existing mature SFT branch fails all six executable held-out Python modules under this new context-rich protocol. The queued full-rehearsal curriculum now adds these original tasks with explicit repeat accounting and matched before/after full-language loss plus supported module tests. Extra retention guards cannot substitute for broad original coding/knowledge/math/image assessments or an independent final benchmark.

Masked reconstruction and caption pooling did not establish useful native vision. The next paired experiment applies original human object-label supervision directly to the own image encoder, avoiding dependence on immature frozen language features as its training target. It combines boxes from a frozen prefix of30K additional admitted photos with prior training photos, yielding14,508 class-balanced-candidate crops and the same61 class choices/221 held crops as the earlier feature probe. Both a frozen encoder plus learned linear head and a trainable encoder/head receive identical sampling, augmentation and update budgets. The frozen branch reaches12.22% correct-image accuracy (macro6.55%) versus7.69% wrong-image target matches; the trainable branch is active. Oracle boxes and a discarded auxiliary head distinguish this task from detection or native language answers, so improved classification alone would not justify transferring the encoder. Matched native alignment/reading and regression evidence remain necessary.

Direct supervised encoder learning improved the fixed oracle-crop classifier from27/221 to63/221, with macro accuracy6.55%→21.85% and a paired parent-bootstrap gain interval10.31–22.42 percentage points. Blank inputs match10/221 original targets and wrong-class/other-parent images0/221. The result supports a representation benefit under this task/budget, without proving optimal frozen-head fitting or generic native vision. The follow-up transfers only this own vision encoder into the original2K-update language snapshot, keeping the original matched caption training and all non-vision tensors fixed. Native retrieval, reading and object answers remain the relevant unresolved question; no donor is promoted from classification scores alone.


Complete module generation is now checked beyond supported Python literals. The pinned original C++ track supplies 18 held-out library exercises, including multi-file implementations, templates/classes and exception interfaces. All original references pass all enabled publisher tests (265 case records, 326 assertions). Compile/runtime confinement precedes execution; original test bytes are preserved and reference JUnit inventories reject missing tests or premature successful exit. Actual compiled failure and filesystem-isolation probes plus a saved-inventory round trip passed. This measures published library behavior rather than efficiency, all repositories/languages or adversarial score-forgery resistance; prior semantic pretraining overlap is not exhaustively audited. A current own-model baseline is running, and the waiting full-rehearsal handoff now requires matched before/after C++ module retention in addition to all existing capability and offline human-feedback guards. An aggregate improvement cannot hide losing an existing correct module. Goal completion remains unproven.


The own mature coding baseline completed all18 C++ module prompts with zero valid complete-file deliveries. Generated responses often used malformed Python, so no candidate reached C++ compilation under this protocol. This reveals an instruction/interface/language-generation gap rather than an isolated algorithm-efficiency measurement. Original source-grounded multi-language exercise instruction training is still queued; subsequent matched assessments must establish any useful change. All202 implementation tests pass, but this does not demonstrate model capability.


The own mature coding checkpoint returned malformed Python instead of complete C++ files on all18 module prompts. The next separate experiment therefore trains on original problem descriptions, starter interfaces and complete reference modules across Python/JavaScript/Go/Java/Rust/C++, preserving all599 implementations. Its fixed300-update schedule visits every implementation four times, plus four recorded batch-remainder exposures, for1,404,899 supervised target tokens. Training targets fit the own4096-token context intact (maximum4073); language-only optimization must preserve every vision tensor exactly. Baselines reuse earlier scores only with exact current source/tokenizer/evaluator provenance; supported Python execution, all18 publisher C++ suites, full181-validation response loss with language groups and both local correctness/efficiency protocols run before/after. No production promotion follows this isolated probe, and broader regression/independent-final evidence remains necessary.

[CodeGen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.13474) studies natural-language/programming-language training and reports improved synthesis when specifications are split into multiple prompts. That motivates examining the instruction/interface gap separately from raw token loss; the current probe keeps a single-turn protocol and is not a reproduction of their multi-turn result. [Mekala et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10430) study instruction-data selection using learning behavior, including a350M selector and larger pretrained models. Our next inference is to measure per-language learning before choosing adaptive sampling; it does not establish that their selection results transfer to this own undertrained706M model. Both sources are research context only: no external pretrained weights or teacher-generated training targets are introduced.


The paired native object-supervision follow-up completed without a usable native gain: image-to-text retrieval4/128→1/128, text-to-image3/128 unchanged, OCR0/170 and object answers0/164 in both branches. Caption NLL2.7723→2.7617 and lower OCR character error do not establish useful decoding. Exact language/critic preservation and matched cases/budgets were verified. The positive oracle-crop classifier result did not transfer to these native response metrics under this fixed language/alignment budget. No production promotion occurred; the main broader-caption/word/data curriculum remains separate.


The original C++ source-target audit separates data quality from weak model output.71/75 training modules pass3,134 published assertions (874 case records); four remain outside the fixed runner's library/preprocessor/thread/dependency scope. The newer Catch3 reference required its exact original bundled sources and read-only kernel entropy access; its earlier filesystem-blocked startup was not an algorithm error. This supplemental audit leaves the fixed18-task held evaluator unchanged. The completed300-update six-language SFT run lowers every language group's held response NLL (overall1.1396→0.8102), while Python remains0/6 functional with syntax4/6→2/6. C++ full-file delivery rises0→7/18, but none compile or pass tests; generated errors include Rust-style `.is_empty()` on `std::string` and nonexistent `std::null`. Thus fitting source likelihood and output labels has not established type/API/algorithm correctness, even where training references are executable. Larger raw-code/instruction learning, stronger language conditioning and measured compiler-feedback interventions remain hypotheses; local before/after function and efficiency assessment is still in progress. No automatic promotion or broad capability conclusion is justified.


The completed module probe lowers source-response likelihood loss but yields no local algorithm/efficiency gain and worsens syntax in both function formats and supported Python modules. Therefore language/file-format fitting does not establish usable code generation. The active longer source mix contains documented CodeSearchNet/Wikipedia; the earlier214M-token broad repository corpus is omitted from that continuation, though it was previously trained. A guarded next-stage hypothesis restores broad-code rehearsal and adds30.7M new training tokens from12 original systems/library repositories, with clear language metadata and preserved publisher-license/source evidence. This is not a claim that data omission alone caused the errors. Raw-code continuation is followed by full supervised instruction/image/dialogue rehearsal to address potential forgetting, and final matched capability/preference guards retain the original main policy if the candidate regresses. No pretrained weights, external teacher or automatic high-end-capability claim is introduced.


The new code corpus has independent source-byte and exact-token-stream evidence: all12 pinned archives reconstruct7,992 original files/9,193 chunks, preserved metadata connects prepared text to those exact chunks, and both packed streams match independent own-tokenizer output. This verifies preparation rather than learning. The continuation handoff also requires per-update actual target receipts, explicitly recording unused packed tail positions and repeated last-batch windows, so a nominal completed-step status cannot substitute for the declared training exposure budget.


The next visual supplement targets compositional reasoning with original CLEVR scene graphs/programs, rather than treating caption likelihood as sufficient evidence. All selected45,120 labels agree with independent symbolic execution. Original official splits, pinned archive identity, exact pixels and per-case controls support a matched developmental assessment; synthetic renderings, bounded image indices and reused tests prevent a generic/independent high-end claim. Original AI2D remains unadmitted because its bundled2016 license conflicts with the current registry declaration; its letter-label questions also require a defined image transform. No external model teacher or pretrained image/language weights are introduced.

The scratch CNN/byte-reader path now supplies measured complete-photo text information: on the same400 development images, own reader refinement raises correctly located/transcribed words2127→2454/11034 while losing131 earlier word targets. This is limited reading, not question answering. [M4C](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.06258) combines text representations with visual/question information and iterative pointer-based answer decoding. That motivates our next hypothesis: condition the own language policy on bounded text/box predictions from our own scratch components while retaining the full image and original question. The prepared `own_ocr_context` interface is simpler than M4C and does not reproduce its architecture or results. No external OCR weights or teacher answers are used. Proper/blank/different-photo controls must regenerate their own predicted context, and matched human-question evaluation is required before claiming a native benefit. Current candidate tradeoffs and fixed word/context budgets remain explicit; no native policy is yet promoted from crop/full-photo word scores.

The actual same-policy128-photo question comparison scored zero exact answers in all plain/empty-schema/own-predicted-context conditions and image controls. Prior plain generations reproduced exactly, so the new context experiment did not expose an original-baseline discrepancy. In30 cases the expected word was present in the own predictions; nevertheless outputs continued earlier scene-description patterns or malformed context values. This does not fully isolate context sufficiency, but demonstrates that supplying recognized text does not itself create question-following/answer selection in the frozen specialist policy. Future conditioning must use actual original human questions/answers, retain broader instruction/code/image rehearsal and test both no-context and predicted-context operation. Word-only scores and context-target coverage cannot replace native generated-answer evidence; no candidate was promoted.

Original-human conditioning data is now finalized and reconstructed for every original question, with 5,858 own-context training variants alongside the unchanged plain questions. The joint stage schedules matched compact-context native assessments using fixed own components and retains all broader rehearsal/guards. Subsequent feedback selection now evaluates the same conditioned task for both DPO and any admitted PPO branch, so text-only human preference optimization cannot silently approve loss of a measured conditioned answer. It requires all 128 original cases, unchanged control prompts/pixels/component identities and consistent generated-answer scores; an aggregate gain cannot hide a lost case or override another failed capability check. These are reused development retention criteria. They do not prove generic visual reasoning, fresh human preference for this policy or frontier-model equivalence; actual joint/feedback training and independent final evaluation remain required.

The matched 7,000-update continuation assessment improves code/Wikipedia likelihood and Python syntax while preserving all frozen vision tensors. Functional code remains zero in both generation formats, and JavaScript syntax remains zero. Generated knowledge answers rise 15→25/559 while forced-choice accuracy falls 13.42%→11.81%, with its interval including chance. This does not show broad semantic competence from more document exposure; current instructions and later joint/feedback stages remain untrained. A typical code response returns `sum(xs)` while omitting the requested even-number condition, and instruction responses can continue documentation rather than supply a function. Further source-pattern fitting alone cannot be treated as a working solution.

The next additional supervision source is the original [NQ-Open release](https://github.com/google-research-datasets/natural-questions/tree/master/nq_open), whose task uses real search questions and human-annotation-derived Wikipedia answer strings. The publisher's data license is CC BY-SA 3.0, rather than the repository code license. All 87,925 training and 3,610 original development rows are pinned and their source blobs verified; no EfficientQA model-submission answer fields are imported. The prepared brief-answer instructions preserve original questions and all reference aliases, with full own-encoder targets and held-question separation. The hypothesis is that explicit human question/answer supervision improves question following and factual decoding beyond document likelihood. It remains untested: new-dev exposure to previous/queued training, source reconstruction, closed-book generated answers, broader retention and independent evaluation must precede a capability claim. The original open-format data lacks page identity, so exact question separation does not establish article or semantic independence.

Independent original-source/encoder reconstruction now passes all 91,535 QA rows. The broader exposure scanner already found one original development question in older packed training text; direct token-window decoding confirms the actual source, so official split identities are insufficient evidence of unseen evaluation here. The full scan remains live across packed, supervised, caption and human-preference inputs. Joint admission requires its completed inventory and preserves all prior rehearsal. Native before/after assessment keeps all 3,610 original cases and exposes the exact-match group separately from the no-exact-match group, rather than silently filtering a benchmark after observing model scores. A matched strict alias scorer and per-case guard are prepared; no QA training or new native capability result exists yet. These remain development controls, not a substitute for independent broad evaluation.

That scan completed with 18 potential exact-question exposures across the complete declared past/current/queued inputs. Three occur in older/queued sources; fifteen original development queries are substrings of longer original NQ training queries, despite zero equality of canonical official train/dev question identities. Direct packed-window and actual encoder-token decoding independently confirm every finding. All 3,610 cases are retained, and 3,592 no-exact-match cases are explicitly qualified rather than declared independently unseen. Real training admission now passes all completed source/input/exposure gates. Factual QA retention is also added to both DPO and reward-gated PPO selection, alongside all existing capability and image-reading criteria, so later alignment cannot silently approve loss of a measured fact answer. No learning gain is yet measured; original references can be ambiguous or dated, and this remains a development benchmark rather than current all-domain correctness or frontier equivalence.

All 37 JavaScript reference modules now pass their exact publisher suites under pinned Node/Jest, including formerly skipped registered cases. This extends executable evidence beyond isolated function prompts and the existing Python/C++ module checks: candidate delivery must preserve named ES-module interfaces, parse, and satisfy the full original behavioral inventories. The queued joint-learning guard cannot approve a lost original module behind an equal aggregate score. The completed own six-language prototype supplies 16 complete file responses and eight parsable modules out of 37, but no full module passes. One lacks a required export; seven fail actual behavior cases. Examples expose task semantics rather than merely syntax: pangram becomes an empty-text check, and sum-of-multiples becomes a one-argument numeric lookup. Repeating a short specialist learning probe is therefore not supported as a route to stronger general code. The ongoing larger foundation and queued instruction/joint stages require these actual generated-module before/after measurements. Successful references and confinement probes establish only the evaluation machinery. Original development suites and same-process assertions cannot certify independent final generalization, adversarial robustness, universal JavaScript support or asymptotic optimality.

The later alignment stage now schedules the same complete JavaScript contract for both DPO and any reward-gated PPO branch. Reusing selected-policy evidence requires exact policy, original-source/test, dependency and evaluator/runtime identities. Positive offline human preference likelihood cannot override a lost original module, and source/interface or incomplete execution inventories are rejected. The completed failed prototype passes this evidence-schema check without implying capability; future-branch learning and native assessment have not started. This closes an evaluation gap between supervised joint learning and feedback selection, while broader functional and independent final generalization remain unproved.

A full actual-Encoder audit now rules out code-target clipping as a general explanation for weak coding: all 897,452 documented code rows retain complete prompts and code labels under the current allocation. Two long general human demonstrations are clipped and kept explicit. Parsing all JavaScript source targets reveals a narrower data defect: using the first brace to delimit a signature drops object-destructured/default parameters in 982 tasks. Corrected prompt variants preserve every original source-code target and metadata field, and independently verified full token sequences fit without clipping. These are prepared training variants rather than executed/validated implementations or measured learning gains; admission remains pending. Broader incomplete module context, source-code semantic validity and the dominant prototype behavior failures remain unresolved, and Ruby multi-line prefix heuristics require separate syntax-aware investigation.

The parameter correction is now admitted to the later joint stage after fresh parsing, full original-source reconstruction, exact own-tokenizer/loss-mask verification and source-file validation separation. All prior rehearsal survives, and native before/after module checks remain required. This offers explicit object/destructured parameter contracts instead of incomplete headers without inventing implementations or teacher judgments. Because the underlying code may depend on omitted surrounding modules and the failed prototype has broader semantic deficits, the correction is a limited training-quality intervention, not evidence that the model now writes correct or efficient code. Actual future generated behavior and independent final evaluation remain necessary.

The earlier raw parenthesis/multi-line indicators are now resolved with complete syntax-aware Go/Ruby source scans. First-brace truncation omits Go parameter/result interface or struct types in 11,435 instructions. For Ruby, 35,571 prompts need complete-header restoration; 31,594 include actual method-body source in the signature context. This provides a concrete distinction between an API-information deficit and easy target-copying context, while keeping six unsupported forms explicit. All 47,006 corrected variants preserve original source labels and attribution, fit full actual Encoder allocation, and reconstruct independently before later joint admission. They add supervised task information and remove implementation prefixes without new teacher judgments or external model weights. Algorithms, surrounding dependency contracts and subsequent model gains are still unverified; native execution and independent generalization remain required rather than inferred from parse/label integrity.

Executable module assessment now extends to Go using all 42 publisher references and all original direct test files. The initially missing generated helper illustrates why declared configuration lists alone do not establish complete source-test coverage: exact pinned archive inspection restores the helper and all references pass without excluding the exercise. The Go path measures file delivery, full compilation and original native behavior, rather than substituting source parsing for execution. Matched before/after development-module retention is now scheduled after joint learning. The completed own prototype assessment is still running, and native Go efficiency/asymptotic guarantees, adversarial reporting robustness and independent final generalization remain unverified.

Full Go native module retention now also gates both future DPO and any reward-gated PPO candidate. The exact selected policy report can be reused only with matching original source/module/test identities, fixed generation budget, verified toolchain and evaluator/runtime; incomplete or inconsistent native reports fail admission. No aggregate gain can hide a lost previously correct Go module or override an older code/reading/factual/preference failure. This preserves the supervised-stage Go criterion through later alignment without claiming a learned improvement. The actual completed-prototype assessment is still running; no future feedback policy or independent all-code behavior has yet been measured.

The completed Go prototype assessment adds native compilation evidence to the earlier JavaScript result: only12/42 responses deliver complete named files, none compile and no full Go suite passes. Repeated declaration loops, missing imports/names and wrong argument contracts show that a short mixed-language source replay did not produce reliable Go. This rules out treating low source-response loss or correct reference modules as successful own-code behavior. Main foundation and queued instruction/joint learning remain the next meaningful policy changes. The real-photo question collection is also now unblocked at its actual disk reserve through verified lossless archival of an inactive historical checkpoint; resumed collection changes actual available photo evidence but does not establish learned grounding.

Original paired-photo human questions now have a finalized-source admission path and scheduled native retention through joint learning and later feedback. A live-journal preview reconstructed 206 questions across103 complete candidate pairs, with original creator declarations, actual stored photo pixels and full response-only Encoder masks checked. Final source/split/perceptual screening is still the collector's responsibility; preview rows are not admitted. Correct/blank/counterpart controls and both-answers-correct pair credit test dependence on actual photos while retaining every case selected by the frozen evaluator, up to128 pairs. The selection guard recomputes aggregate and original human-answer scores and rejects lost answers even when total accuracy stays equal. All older code, reading, factual and preference checks remain required. The main training and collector processes were preserved during verified reloads of idle controllers. This connects generic-photo evidence to future learning and measurement; it does not demonstrate learned grounding or high-end capability.

The10000-update check adds evidence that foundation prediction improves without establishing functional code ability: held code/Wikipedia NLL continue downward to0.787/2.659, while both coding modes still fail all24 functional/efficiency tasks. Instruction syntax and generated-choice quiz scores regress from the7000 checkpoint. The full pre-warmup/warmup/7000/10000 trajectory is retained, so a favorable adjacent interval does not hide earlier stronger syntax or quiz scores. More source-code prediction alone has not yet delivered instruction-following algorithms.

To test parameter reuse, a separate architecture retains all24 original layer tensors and re-executes a12-layer core with independently learned pass gates and independent stage caches. It adds three parameters and permits60 layer executions at706M total weights. A production-size own-weight CUDA trial learned only the gates; its0.0115% held loss reduction is too small and indirect to support coding improvement. [Recurrent-depth language modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05171) supports investigating more computation per weight; [tiny recursive reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04871) studies restricted puzzle tasks and does not demonstrate broad language/coding parity. Our next meaningful architecture comparison requires full shared-backbone learning, explicit input/depth conditioning alternatives and matched compute/target budgets, followed by executed-code and multimodal retention tests.

The new public corpus supplies complete original source files across16 OS/network/crypto/security projects, with52.6M new training tokens and6.3M validation tokens verified by exact own-tokenizer reconstruction. All earlier245.1M code tokens are preserved in the queued continuation. License declarations, source/archive identities, exact benchmark filters and per-file split grouping are recorded, without claiming semantic independence or universal coverage. The native Rust reference backend also passes all24 original integration suites with355 cases after calibrating a finite deadline against the complete unchanged references; generated own-policy Rust behavior is still unmeasured. These additions address data variety and future measurement, not achieved frontier-level code competence.

A full shared-backbone continuation now tests the gated recurrent prototype with both a shared-target control and an equal decoder-layer/token-execution control. Wall time, total FLOPs and optimizer/head computation are reported separately from that proxy budget. Ordinary256-update training already lowers held prediction loss without improving functional code, so any recurrence benefit must be established against those controls and actual generated behavior. All policies are isolated from the live main curriculum; unchanged vision tensors alone do not establish retained image answering.

The completed comparison shows that this gated repeat did not earn a larger run: recurrent NLL 1.688485 is effectively tied with the equal-target ordinary control's 1.688606, while the equal-layer/token-budget ordinary control reaches 1.615256. All generated functional and efficiency scores remain zero. Extra recurrent passes also slightly worsen loss compared with the recurrent branch's own one-pass output. Its gates remain near zero, consistent with—but not proof of—learning to ignore the extra passes. These results concern one short own-weight continuation and do not refute recurrent architectures in general. The measured policy files and full execution reports are retained; no candidate is selected.

Inspection of the original [recurrent-depth paper's architecture and failed runs](https://arxiv.org/html/2502.05171v2) gives a concrete next design distinction: its recurrence reinjects input features through a learned adapter and uses post-residual normalization. The authors report that conventional pre-normalization can learn to ignore recurrence; adding iteration embeddings also harmed their depth extrapolation. Our gated repeat preserves the own base and causal caches, but is not a replication of that architecture. If the current controlled trial does not show benefit, input-conditioned normalized recurrence is a justified next hypothesis, not a capability already achieved or evidence that a sub1B model can match a frontier system.

The separate completed Rust policy assessment confirms a language-level deficit in the earlier six-language prototype: only four of 24 responses deliver complete original file sets, and none compile. Actual compiler diagnostics show repeated symbols, nonexistent methods and incompatible return types despite visible original starter interfaces. All unchanged reference suites and the 11 compiled restriction/report probes pass, so successful evaluation machinery is not confused with learned coding. These are reused development modules, not independent final evaluation or a current-main score. Full instruction learning and a better recurrence design remain future interventions; neither has been proved to solve the deficit.

The next implemented candidate adds learned concatenated input/state conditioning and post-residual RMS normalization to the repeated core, removing scalar pass bypass. It preserves own canonical weights but changes their computation; training-window-only activation calibration approximately retains their residual scales. Its total parameter count is 710.77M. Initial four-pass loss is higher than one-pass loss, which prevents treating the architectural change itself as an improvement. A larger full-language continuation now compares recurrent and ordinary versions of this same modified architecture at equal target exposure and equal decoder-layer/token budgets. Published recurrence findings motivate this experiment, but it uses our own transferred weights and bounded continuation rather than replicating their full random-initialization training recipe.

The original Rust module criterion now survives both supervised policy selection and subsequent offline alignment. Entire source populations, successful case inventories and fixed native budgets are matched; aggregate ties cannot conceal a lost correct module. A model's invalid generated package is a recorded contract failure, rather than a reason to stop all later assessments. The previous prototype runtime is preserved separately. These pipeline additions establish future measurement/retention; they do not turn the existing zero native Rust passes into learned capability.

The completed conditioned recurrence experiment provides a more specific result than the earlier near-zero gates. Its trained one/two/four-pass losses are 1.889613/1.735413/1.738857, so input conditioning and residual normalization permit useful prediction computation in additional passes. However, the equal-target single-pass control is better at 1.624056, and the equal-layer/token-budget control reaches 1.525301; no trained branch passes any of the 24 functional or efficiency tasks. This is not evidence for replacing the production architecture. It is a one-seed continuation with fixed four-pass training, not a test of the published stochastic-depth/full-pretraining recipe.

Generated response inspection also identifies source specialization: shorter ordinary training introduces C++/Rust-style expressions into Python, and longer ordinary training produces repeated comments and quote runs. This sharpens the distinction between source prediction and task-following code. Full earlier-code rehearsal and language-conditioned instruction targets remain necessary, and native C++ execution is now assessing the exact retained policies to avoid drawing an all-language conclusion solely from Python/JavaScript. Earlier verified-code training did learn 20 of 32 seen Python source programs with 99.39% teacher-forced token accuracy, while its held-task performance remained weak; that training-only result supports learning/memorization, not generalization.

The completed native C++ check rules out a working-function gain on these 12 local contracts: initial, ordinary 1,024, ordinary 2,560 and recurrent 1,024 policies all fail every functional and efficiency case.
