Two shorter-control translation units pass syntax because extracted code is empty; they omit the required callable functions and fail linking. All original references pass, and every actual policy state, signature/case population and evaluator/runtime identity is verified. The recurrent architecture has learned a useful extra-pass prediction transform, but it does not beat its ordinary controls or produce correct generated programs in these evaluations. Further recurrence pretraining recipes remain untested; the immediate learning priority is language-conditioned instruction generalization with full broad-code rehearsal, rather than promoting this candidate on prediction loss.
<!-- Public systems-code recovery, 2026-10-14 -->

After temporary instance storage was lost, the OS/network/security collection was recollected into persistent workspace storage rather than treated as byte-identical historical data. The new 16-project generation reconstructs 11,923 complete original files and verifies 52,970,300 new training tokens plus 6,239,932 validation tokens with our own tokenizer. All 245,123,134 earlier code training tokens remain in its queued continuation. Commit, archive, license and original-file identities are recorded independently of the lost generation. Exact benchmark filters and file-group splits remain limited checks against contamination and do not establish semantic independence. No generated-code improvement from this new generation has been measured.

Main training recovered its original optimizer/RNG and frozen plan at update 12,000 after losing progress logged through 12,480. The separate instruction-generalization trial had not reached baseline assessment or learning; failed recurrence experiment reports survive, but their temporary policy files do not. Native JavaScript/Rust runtime reconstruction changed bookkeeping, publisher metadata and rebuilt library bytes, so the original evidence was preserved and every publisher reference suite and actual restriction probe was rerun before restoring the waiting joint/feedback controllers. All 37 JavaScript and 24 Rust reference modules pass with the same original reference, test and case identities. This recovery restores the ability to learn and evaluate; it supplies no new evidence of frontier-level capability or of reliable model-generated systems code.
The persistent instruction retry starts from the exact own12,000-update foundation and admits2,275,528 complete response targets over6,000 example exposures. Its completion baseline has9/24 syntax passes but no functional or efficiency passes; instruction baseline has none on any criterion. These results continue to favor investigating task-following learning before promoting source-prediction or recurrence improvements. Both final learning outcomes and image behavior remain unmeasured.

Reviewing the original [multi-token prediction paper, Table S7 and Appendix D](https://arxiv.org/html/2404.19737v1) also argues against assuming that larger-model headlines apply to our budget. At0.6B parameters, MBPP pass@1 is4.7 for next-token prediction versus4.6/3.0 for two/four-token prediction. Its additional-head finetuning experiment reports no significant overall improvement. These published settings differ from ours; our inference is to defer this intervention pending stronger local evidence, rather than treat it as a demonstrated sub1B coding gain. No multi-token variant was implemented or trained here.
Checking every original JavaScript/Go/Rust training reference against complete publisher suites provides an actionable data distinction. Some failures are real context defects: Go exemplars require publisher editor-file definitions that were absent from prompts, and relocating a JavaScript proof out of.meta/ requires updating its relative imports. Others arise from a restricted evaluator: external Rust crates, ESM mocking, filesystem fixtures, skipped/example-only Go cases, and paasio's explicit two-thread requirement. The latter passes all13 cases when that requirement is supplied, without changing test bytes. One JavaScript crypto-square example also fails its published54-character result. These causes are recorded separately; unsuccessful restricted assessment is not automatically an incorrect program.

The corrected source generation reconstructs596 rows, adds12 complete public support contexts, relocates five static imports in one target, and excludes three inspected JavaScript cases from the focused instruction mixture. Its entire prompt/response masks fit4096 tokens without truncation. Eight additional Go references and the corrected JavaScript module pass complete original suites. The initial instruction trial was stopped after100 updates with no selected policy; the corrected1,500-update run starts again from the same own12,000 foundation, reusing its exact completed baselines. Corrected source is also admitted into future joint rehearsal, with every original validation population and retained capability criterion preserved.

The corrected trial has now completed2,268,351 response targets and all five matched after-assessments. Completion syntax rises9/24 to14/24 and instruction syntax0/24 to12/24, but both retain zero functional/efficiency passes; native C++ retains zero on12 contracts. Held module response NLL improves14.83%, while generated quiz accuracy worsens33/559 to20/559. Likelihood selection rises only71/559 to72/559 and remains statistically compatible with chance. The verified case-level comparison records lost as well as gained correctness and separates strict letter-only answers from the existing permissive parser. These measurements support better source-token prediction and some syntactic task formatting, with no demonstrated algorithmic correctness or broad knowledge gain. Neither vision behavior nor human preference was measured, and no candidate is promoted.

The verified-Python exposure audit additionally found587 visited algorithms out of669: several algorithms share a publisher file, and the old schedule erroneously used that file group as its algorithm identity. A separate future scheduler groups by original program SHA while retaining file identities for source separation. On the exact accepted pools, all669 originals precede repeated variants with unchanged example allocation. This fixes coverage for a future experiment; it does not retroactively repair the completed trial or explain all functional failures. A new paired128-program diagnostic restricts selection to actually exposed original-name sources, verifies every public positive against its published examples, and measures teacher forcing separately from free generation. The examples are training material with visible tests, so any resulting success is learning/memorization evidence rather than generalization.

[DPO](https://arxiv.org/html/2305.18290v2) optimizes preferred-versus-rejected relative policy/reference likelihoods; its successful alignment experiments do not demonstrate algorithmic learning in our weak own model. [SimPO](https://arxiv.org/html/2405.14734v3) uses average response log probability and a margin, but its main comparisons use7B/8B model families and later9B models. [Normalized Rewards for Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.16240v1) examines likelihood displacement and regularization. These sources motivate separately tracking preferred and rejected likelihoods and including a supervised-positive control; they do not establish that a specific preference method improves this706M model. No published model weights or external generated teaching targets are used.

The next source preparation therefore separates wrong output values from syntax, missing-callable, restricted-module and runtime failures. A positive must still pass every visible public example, and the own negative must reproducibly reach the callable and return a mismatched value. Original prompt/program/exposure/model/runtime identities and full loss masks are checked. The paired source diagnostic has now completed and produced83 executable wrong-result contrasts; these are untrained synthetic execution labels, not human preference data.

The128-program seen-source result shows substantial token learning with limited free-generation correctness. Syntax improves6 to124, teacher-forced accuracy76.25% to95.10%, and response NLL0.999339 to0.187312. Published-example success improves0 to8 programs, while both held coding formats remain0/24 and native C++ remains0/12. For example, generated even-number code tests negativity instead of parity, and generated Fibonacci code squares a triangular-number expression. These literal errors and the83 runnable wrong-value generations support testing stronger positive learning and an execution contrast objective against a matched supervised control. They do not establish generalization, efficiency or frontier capability.

A separate gradient implementation evaluates the supervised-positive plus DPO objective through detached scalar derivatives, then backpropagates each response separately. For deterministic forwards this is the same chain-rule gradient as retaining both computation graphs; seven independent normal-objective comparisons verify it with unequal response lengths and checkpointing. Full own-model backward readiness confirms finite language gradients, frozen vision/critic,6.38GiB peak allocation for the tested pair and exact unchanged model tensors. Optimizer memory and actual learning are not measured by this readiness check.

Both branches of the controlled learning experiment have completed eight passes over83 own wrong-value contrast pairs, alongside identical six-language module and public human-instruction rehearsal. Positive/module/human NLL weights are1/0.25/0.25; the anchored branch additionally uses0.1-weight DPO with beta0.1 and frozen own-reference scores. Both use the same664-update schedule, learning-rate trajectory, own starting state and complete target masks. More compute and rejected response targets are present only in the anchored branch, so its possible benefit would not establish equal-compute superiority. All original held/code/knowledge populations and the128 seen-source population are being evaluated, with no automatic adoption. The supervised control retains zero functional/efficiency passes on both24-task Python/JavaScript formats and12 native C++ contracts; completion syntax changes14 to13 and instruction syntax12 to11. Held module NLL worsens1.80%, generated quiz answers worsen20 to14 of559, and likelihood selection remains72/559. Thus its much lower positive training loss does not establish held coding or knowledge improvement. These are reused development tasks, not independent final benchmarks. The preference-trained branch's outcomes and the remaining paired assessments are still pending.

Generated undefined-variable, input-type and callable-name failures motivate a distinct hypothesis: broader explicit argument binding may help task following beyond repeated original names. A restricted static transform now supplies1,217 argument-name variants across612 admitted public algorithms. It preserves source descriptions/examples/licenses and file groups, checks inverse AST structure, and reexecutes each original and transformed program against every published example. These are synthetic transformations and training material; their correctness checks neither establish universal semantics nor demonstrate learning. A future1,500-update schedule prioritizes the new argument variants after visiting every one of669 originals, exposing758 binding examples while retaining matched module/human choices. The earlier sorted-variant schedule would expose zero of the appended argument variants. This preparation resolves data/exposure readiness; no current controller uses it and no model benefit is claimed.

The completed supervised control provides direct evidence of source learning alongside forgetting. Training-source published-example success rises8 to86 of128, and all83 additionally supervised programs become correct. Yet all eight previously correct sources become wrong, and held functional/efficiency results remain zero. Aggregate training success therefore conceals complete loss of the original successes. The next focused intervention needs broader original-code rehearsal and case-level retention tracking alongside its binding hypothesis. All128 original public training programs have now been copied unchanged into a dedicated rehearsal source and rechecked against every published example. This preparation does not train a policy, restore the lost programs, prove generalization or select a production model.

Both controlled branches are now fully assessed. The anchored policy passes87 training-source programs versus86 for the supervised control, preserving two of the eight old successes while the control preserves none. Both learn all83 additionally supervised programs, but neither passes any held functional/efficiency task in either coding format or native C++. The anchored branch has worse generated knowledge accuracy (12 versus14 of559) and module response loss (0.799067 versus0.796401). These results do not establish useful held coding gain from the extra preference computation; they also do not prove that preference optimization cannot help a stronger policy or other training settings.

The next single continuation starts from the less-specialized original instruction policy. Its1,500 updates cover every original algorithm, prioritize the new argument-name variants, preserve six-language module/public human-instruction exposure and add explicit rehearsal over all128 original public source programs. The objective is token-mean NLL over the four main examples plus0.25 rehearsal NLL, with unchanged own706M parameters and frozen vision/critic. Source/target budgets are checked before optimization and every100 updates. Actual optimizer updates are now running, and all six matched assessments plus case-level lost-success tracking follow completed training. The intervention changes several aspects of data/exposure simultaneously, so a positive result would support the combined continuation rather than isolate argument-name augmentation. No image/human-quality or frontier capability is established by implementation tests or this initial progress.

That continuation has now completed all1,500 updates and its durable checkpoint is verified. Its first five development assessments show no held functional/efficiency passes, worse module response loss (0.879539 versus0.782355), and lower generated/likelihood knowledge counts (15/65 versus20/72 of559). Final training-source retention is still being assessed. These findings do not support adopting the combined continuation for broad capability; neither low rehearsal loss nor full source coverage establishes generalization.

The broader multimodal work also had a concrete resource bottleneck: original paired-photo collection waited below35GiB despite sufficient space for the measured known writers. A verified26GiB option now covers23.07GiB of maximum simultaneous writes plus safety, while retaining the original1.5GiB photo cap, source identities, current licensing/attribution requirements, publisher pacing and complete pair/split protections. Only idle collection/waiting consumers were reloaded; active model training and all future capability guards remain intact. Source requests have resumed after cooldown, but additional licensed images and improved visual understanding remain unproven.

The complete source-learning check now establishes128/128 published-example successes and retention of all eight old successes, with100% teacher-token accuracy and0.001551 source NLL. Thus the added broader rehearsal resolves forgetting on these examined sources. It leaves held coding at zero and worsens held knowledge/module measures, so neither source fit nor retained successes support adopting this candidate for general capability.

An inference-only diagnosis is now separating two concrete cues. On the same128 known algorithms and exact completed policy, one condition removes the machine-added example block; another also assigns a new function identifier and rewrites the gold callable/recursion. Original descriptions, parameters and scoring examples remain, and every transformed public reference must still pass. The model receives none of the removed appended cases, while those same cases remain the scoring oracle. This can reveal prompt/name dependence of memorized programs but cannot establish unseen-algorithm competence, isolate every prompt-position effect or guarantee complete removal of examples from original human prose. No new model learning occurs in this diagnosis.

Inspection exposed a concrete qualification:74 of the128 original descriptions still contain parseable doctests after removing the appended block. The first condition passes118 programs, while the requested-name condition stops because some original prose repeats the target declaration. Its partial evidence is preserved without a full renamed-population score. The revised diagnosis removes both normal and comment-prefixed Python doctests and renames every repeated requested declaration consistently. All256 reference checks pass. Fifteen original descriptions retain other identifier mentions, and some become underspecified without examples, so the revised result is still a known-source cue/contract diagnosis rather than proof of novel semantic generalization. The corrected inference-only experiment is now live.

Before interpreting the name-condition results, a structural qualification flags11 of the128 prompts that retain only declarations and standard boilerplate after example removal. Their requested-name outputs cannot establish prose-conditioned semantic competence because the remaining task is underspecified. All128 raw cases remain in the diagnosis; a separate117-program nonempty-prose view will be explicitly qualified, without certifying those remaining descriptions as complete. The full669-algorithm source audit finds36 declaration-only cases, all in the admitted TheAlgorithms/Python population, plus one parse/inspection exception. No source algorithms, rows or live schedules are removed. This distinguishes valuable example-conditioned learning material from evidence of natural-language specification understanding (`reports/public-program-specification-quality-v1.json`).

The corrected first condition is complete: removing appended and recognized Python doctest examples lowers known-source correctness128 to106 of128, with125 syntactically valid outputs. The pre-qualified nonempty-prose group passes99/117; declaration-only prompts pass7/11. Fourteen failures are wrong public-example values, with the remaining failures involving syntax, missing callables, types or runtime names. These results establish sensitivity to the changed prompt on known algorithms, while context length/position and incomplete descriptions limit any causal semantic interpretation. The requested-name condition remains live; no unseen-algorithm competence or model improvement is claimed.

Both corrected conditions are now complete. Adding a new requested identifier yields87/128 correct programs and124 syntactically valid outputs; the nonempty-prose subset passes85/117 and declaration-only prompts2/11. Six of the41 failures lack the requested callable. None supplies a correct original callable when that alternative is checked, without altering the primary score. These results warrant examining body correctness and broader problem generalization rather than assuming an interface-name fix will resolve the gap. They also show some robustness to identifier changes on known algorithms, not competence on unseen algorithms. Independent recomputation preserves all original case identities and qualifies incomplete descriptions (`reports/code-prompt-controls-v2/comparison-independent-check-v1.json`).

The original own instruction policy and binding/rehearsal continuation have now completed the same175-problem public comparison. Both pass zero problems; syntax changes168 to167. The candidate reduces missing requested definitions93 to46, but the unique alternative-callable diagnostic passes only1/46 versus2/85 for the original policy. Those diagnostic successes do not satisfy the requested interface and never alter the primary score. Most failures therefore cannot be resolved by a function-name change alone. Both policies receive the same specification/signature prompt without scoring examples and use fixed greedy512-token generation; original populations, exclusions, source/tokenizer hashes and runtime are preserved. Exact source-reference inspection additionally identifies two identical implementations under different training names (square/rhombus perimeter and Bell numbers); the remaining173 problems also have no primary passes and are not certified free of other overlap. This broader restricted development evidence agrees with the local suites: the combined continuation improves known-source fitting and interface adherence, without establishing broader functional coding gain. All multimodal and human-feedback requirements remain outstanding.

Rather than repeat another learning intervention on the same small program pool immediately, a modest additional explanatory source is prepared from165 original cp-algorithms articles. Human prose, mathematics and code remain together, without converting partial snippets into supposedly verified standalone solutions or generating synthetic teaching labels. A pinned source revision, original CC-BY-SA-4.0 license/attribution and all file groups are retained. The own-tokenizer corpus contains569,996 training and48,805 validation tokens, with exact independent token reconstruction and no cross-split file groups. This expands available teaching material but is not trained or scheduled into the existing live frozen pipeline; its benefit remains a hypothesis, and its scale cannot establish frontier capability.

The next source investigation targets a substantially larger human problem/solution pool, rather than another short replay of the same669 algorithms. The [CodeNet publisher repository](https://github.com/IBM/Project_CodeNet/tree/55c323b527ab3d6510d55edff188bc0a0d7bc5e5) describes original problem statements, multilingual submissions, judge outcomes and duplicate/problem metadata. The [author paper](https://jiechenjiechen.github.io/pub/codenet.pdf), Appendix A.2/A.6, explicitly identifies CDLA-Permissive-2.0 for the dataset and Apache-2.0 for tooling. The current official8.34GB source object is accessible and its download is launched with a pinned ETag, bounded bytes, preserved model-storage reserve and tested conditional range retries. This is source acquisition, not a new policy update or evidence of capability. Subsequent curation must preserve complete problem context and separate whole problem families, use acceptance labels as published judge evidence without claiming universal correctness/efficiency, and retain distinct human-feedback training requirements. The full source archive is neither executed nor unpacked indiscriminately into the workspace.

The complete8,343,137,473-byte source is now downloaded and independently checksummed. Original archive ordering places3999 description files before submissions, while CSV metadata must be reached through the complete archive scan. A bounded streaming inspector is therefore running before selection: it records source counts and preserves original descriptions/metadata plus small derived manifests, without extracting submission bodies. The gzip stream must be drained through its trailer before CRC/integrity completion is reported. A1000-submission fixture also checks that streaming does not accumulate Python tar header objects. Six focused inspection tests pass. This establishes a route to source-grounded problem/solution curation; it does not yet establish an admitted split, checked training programs or any new model learning.

That inspection is now complete:13,916,868 original code members match13,916,868 metadata rows, with7,460,588 original Accepted labels. The complete gzip stream passed its CRC/size check and the final archive digest is unchanged. A description audit exports all3999 original pages, recognizing8910 sample pairs, while retaining940 media-review flags and one malformed-page failure. It catches two practical formatting failures before admission: full-width Japanese heading numbers and explanatory preformatted matrices being misread as sample outputs. Eleven focused checks pass. Comparison against3867 publisher first pairs agrees in3826 cases exactly or under whitespace normalization; the remaining41 are explicit review material.

This source also demonstrates why examples cannot automatically become execution rewards: problemp01662 explicitly shows nonoptimal illustrative graphs rather than valid expected outputs, while the publisher'sp02391 first-output extraction loses the comparison operator. Neither is relabeled as a verified oracle. Publisher notes also distinguish272 unverified first-pair problems and21 problems without accepted references in their four tested languages; those are sample/reference qualifications, not proof that every Accepted submission for those problems is wrong. Original judge status remains evidence about the historical judge, separate from current-runtime correctness or human preferences.

A deterministic metadata pass now selects154,648 Accepted candidates across4032 original problems, retaining up to four distinct anonymized authors per problem/language. It considers every original CSV row and uses a bounded publisher size hint, not generated model scores or a claimed efficiency ordering. Independent artifact checks verify counts, member uniqueness and author diversity; all three focused selection checks pass. Source bodies, original identical/near-duplicate information and full prompt/response contexts remain unprocessed, so no split is assigned and no training begins. This expands the potential supervised pool substantially while preserving the original multimodal/all-round/human-feedback objective and the active frozen training pipeline.

The selected-body pass is now running against the unchanged original archive. It preserves raw source bytes in one indexed store instead of unpacking millions of files, and carries exact member offsets/digests plus encoding/size qualifications forward. Thirty explicit original auxiliary members include identical-problem clusters, clone clusters/graphs, accepted-outlier lists and the source analysis configurations for C, C++, Java and Python. These support subsequent split/duplicate review; they do not certify every language or family clean of overlap. Seven focused checks pass, including a corrupt trailer beyond the selected source prefix and bounded memory for unselected headers. Completion requires every selected body and auxiliary member, full gzip integrity and unchanged original source/runtime inputs. No source code is executed or admitted to model training by this step.

The copy and every indexed body join are now checked:154648 originals and30 auxiliary members are complete, with matching declared sizes, valid UTF-8 and no NUL bytes. Publisher clone paths retain the old `CodeNet` root and require explicit mapping to original archive identities. The strict parser also exposed a logically truncated Python clone file ending at line989186. The original failed run remains preserved; a tested qualification admits only its exact inspected terminal fragment, retains complete prefix relations and explicitly reports4325 selected Python members without publisher clone annotations. This does not repair missing source data or certify it clean.

The proposal combines153 publisher identical-problem clusters, exact selected programme copies and available selected clone relations. It yields3488 components over4053 original problem IDs, with a largest component of240. Structural programme analysis excludes 13 output-only Text submissions and 4 empty/non-program sources while preserving their original bytes. These groups are a basis for subsequent split design; their fuzzy relationships are not semantic equivalence proofs, and earlier pretraining contamination remains uncertified. Ten focused grouping/qualification checks pass, while a separate consistency pass verifies source counts, alias coupling, reported exact-copy coupling and coverage qualifications.

New programme targets also need the full existing text budget. A separate encoder adapter now retains both original sides whenever their complete BOS/prompt/program/EOS sequence fits4096 tokens, including programmes longer than half the window, and rejects actual overflow. Four focused checks verify masks and exact target retention. The live fixed training source is unchanged; original CodeSearchNet instruction preparation already bounds its targets to the legacy cap. This adapter enables future complete CodeNet rows rather than establishing dataset admission or model improvement by itself.

The actual full-context population is now measured: 124,105 of 154,648 original problem/program pairs fit the existing 4096-token window with complete response masks and no truncation. Fixed SHA component roles yield 112,609 unique complete training examples, 5415 validation examples and 5570 test examples after 511 full token/mask duplicates are coupled. All 4053 original problem IDs receive roles; all candidate records and canonical representatives are independently rechecked. This is a reproducible internal source partition, not certified semantic or pretraining-contamination independence.

Executable checking now distinguishes complete stdin/stdout programmes from the earlier named-function contracts. Python runs isolated without site-packages, with an explicit trusted exit/quit bootstrap, under filesystem and syscall restrictions installed before interpreter startup. C++ runs as static ISO C++17/libc++/musl, with a recorded standard-header shim for bits/stdc++.h; source-era GNU extensions can remain unsupported. Input, combined output, memory, CPU and parent wall time are bounded. Original programme bytes stay unchanged. Fourteen focused checks include native static constructors and a regression for an exited compiler whose pipes closed before the parent's next poll. The interrupted v1 pilot and sources remain preserved, and v2 binds the same 64 original references before execution. Whitespace-normalized sample agreement is only reference/runtime/extraction evidence; floats, property judges, alternative valid outputs, hidden cases and efficiency require separate treatment. No model is evaluated or improved by this preparation.

The fixed 64-reference pilot now completes. Its raw results have 56 whitespace-normalized sample agreements, four nonliteral-output reviews and four environment qualifications (two GNU C++ extensions and two NumPy imports). Independent source-specific checks resolve all four output reviews using the retained original HTML: exact rational ticket-subset shortest paths with stated 0.001 tolerance; stated probability tolerance of 10^-6; an exact mean/median gap optimum, checked exhaustively on small distributions; and coin-reachable, soluble, capacity-bounded optimal sugar density. All eight recognized samples from those four problems satisfy their rules. This gives 60 compatible source references without changing the raw diagnostics or calling the four environment limitations wrong programs. Six focused oracle checks pass. The later input-pipe accounting correction has its own regression and the current split/transport group passes 15 checks. Historical runtime source snapshots remain bound to the completed pilot; these preparation results do not score or promote any model.

The complete original-code view is now durable and directly consumable without repeating text truncation or split hashing. Its123,594 unique rows have exact original prompt/programme strings, metadata and compressed provenance alongside313,027,764 bytes of uint16 input IDs and explicit response-start/role offsets. A separate whole-population reconstruction checks programme bytes, source framing, token/mask identity and sealed role for every row. All agree;5843 unique targets exceed the old half-window cap. Five focused reader/writer checks additionally cover padding, unauthorised training access, corruption and self-consistent-but-invalid offsets. The whole artifact occupies380,695,599 bytes and remains unadmitted pending broader source execution inspection.

NumPy-only confinement works in two focused tests, but the two original dependency failures also import Numba. They remain environment qualifications; adding NumPy has not changed the60/64 pilot compatibility count. Broader source-reference checking now covers all17,729 eligible original C++/explicit-Python3 programmes with usable recognized samples in the durable corpus, using two bounded CPU workers after a successful two-language smoke. Original roles are fixed before execution; unsupported runtimes, missing source samples and environment/oracle differences stay explicit. Literal sample agreement or one of four source-specific rules is reference compatibility evidence, not unseen-case correctness or a model score. No new model weights, architecture, human preference labels or frontier-capability claims follow from this preparation.

The original code corpus needs library/runtime qualifications as well as syntax checks. An independent GNU C++17 profile now uses verified official Ubuntu GCC11.4/libstdc++/glibc packages in an owned software root. The signature→package-index→archive chain and extracted file bytes are checked, with exact software archives and signed metadata retained durably. Header/library relocation follows the primary [GCC directory options](https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/Directory-Options.html); static linking follows the [GCC linking options](https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/Link-Options.html). The active interpreter, old ISO-C++ evaluator, source roles and model weights remain unchanged.

Five confinement/resource checks pass for the GNU profile. The original p03058 source reserves approximately1GiB for static bitset storage, so the384MiB function-oriented address-space limit cannot run it even on small samples. A documented1536MiB standalone GNU cap resolves that specific resource qualification without changing original source bytes or filesystem/syscall restrictions. The preserved384MiB pilot remains evidence of the earlier environment mismatch, not incorrect source code. Both original GNU-specific references now pass all six recognized samples. The source pilot therefore has62/64 compatible references across explicitly different recorded profiles; its two Numba-dependent references remain unresolved. This is a composite compatibility inventory, not a uniform-runtime benchmark or an efficiency/model score.

The broader additional GNU pass covers every one of the10,265 predeclared original C++ references, using one CPU worker and fixed source roles. It runs alongside the existing two-worker17,729-reference assessment and four-L40 foundation training. Neither raw profile changes earlier diagnostics or automatically turns compiler/runtime/sample-oracle differences into wrong-code/preference labels. CodeNet admission, actual new-data learning, hidden-case/resource assessments and frontier capability remain unproven.

The source-dependent JIT profile is pinned to NumPy1.26.4, Numba0.59.1 and llvmlite0.42.0, matching the primary [Numba version-support matrix](https://numba.readthedocs.io/en/0.59.1/user/installing.html#version-support-information) for the available Python3.11 interpreter. Published PyPI wheel hashes, exact archives and extracted software files are checked. This creates an additional compatible runtime profile, not a reconstruction of the source-era Python3.8.2 judge or a change to the active model environment.

Eight focused confinement/transport checks pass. Actual cached JIT execution works while writes remain limited to the private temporary cache. Parent monitoring includes visible cache files and open unlinked files, with a64MiB/256-entry stop budget,2MiB per-file cap and32 descriptors; transient exec visibility gets a bounded grace and persistent inspection failure stops execution. Cold JIT/cache startup is included in the30-second sample deadline and is not an algorithmic efficiency measure. The stopped early monitor pilot remains preserved; both original sources then pass all five recognized samples under the final constrained profile.

All24 statically explicit Numba-import programmes in the fixed7464-reference Python cohort were assessed without model updates or outcome-based reselection. Eighteen match their recognized samples. Six require their source-declared native-preparation branch before importing a generated local module; source AST/identity/role evidence records this interface qualification. It does not prove that the preparation phase compiles or justify treating these sources as plain standalone targets. Separately, the original64-reference pilot now has compatible samples across its recorded profiles for every original member. That composite inventory remains distinct from model quality, hidden-case correctness, efficiency, human preference evidence or independent final evaluation. CodeNet training remains unstarted.

The prospective CodeNet objective now separates complete-code token-mean loss from explicitly weighted rehearsal token-mean loss. Coefficients scale the forward loss before backward; the existing accumulation implementation corrects DDP's worker averaging. An analytic comparison passes with two real Gloo workers whose local response counts and microbatch lengths differ. Four further focused checks cover source coverage/resume addressing, explicit rounding, rehearsal rotation and invalid objectives. This validates the optimisation arithmetic without asserting a successful curriculum.

The actual own706M checkpoint was then used for a CPU-only full-size backward audit:2231 complete code response targets, including an answer beyond the legacy2047-token cap, plus26 original image-caption targets. Every one of the218 language tensors has finite nonzero gradients, while vision/critic gradients stay absent and all model-state tensors remain bitwise unchanged. A fully enumerated prospective three-pass sampler covers every fixed training representative while exposing no held role. Its5279 complete global updates include29 disclosed rounding exposures, yielding337,856 code examples and187,907,623 response targets. The complete-code neural driver now has an explicit, fail-closed run-plan interface. Each update computes the code global response-token mean plus a configurable positive coefficient times one rotating replay family's global response-token mean. The prospective coefficient0.5 is a hypothesis, not an established optimum. Deterministic absolute-position permutations and separate family occurrence cursors determine source exposures. Actual replay-family coverage/response-token exposure still needs enumeration against the completed manifest; balancing families does not ensure complete rehearsal of every row.

Twelve focused runner/checkpoint checks include real four-worker CPU execution of the actual update function using a small own decoder/vision architecture, both replay modalities, long complete code targets and frozen vision/critic weights. Restarted weights and Adam state match the uninterrupted CPU execution bitwise. A separate four-worker analytic test checks unequal local code/replay target counts. A stochastic two-worker check separately verifies exact rank-specific random-state recovery. Snapshot overflow keeps the previous checkpoint intact; rank-zero write failure reaches all workers. Final-budget resume may finish the inference artifact without taking additional updates. These are software/CPU arithmetic results, not training gains or full-size GPU evidence.

An initial ordinary-DDP restart test exposed up to3.73e-9 element differences in a recorded tensor after bucket rebuilding. The new path accumulates both objective gradients locally using the existing world/global-target scaling, then performs a single worker average per parameter in fixed order before clipping and Adam. It passes exact CPU restart and the independent global-gradient calculation. CUDA floating-point reproducibility and performance remain unmeasured. Another test exposed unchanged timestamps on rapid same-size rewrites; small bound files are now rehashed each update, while all bound artifacts are completely rehashed at initialization/checkpoints. Tests and initial failures are preserved.

The independent replay audit is chained to the exact live preparation process and will account for every original frozen train-file row as either complete-fit or explicitly over-window. It also reads every fit through the actual offset/ID/mask reader and checks actual normalized448px encoder pixels against original uint8-RGB golden hashes. This audit has not yet finished. Later queued dialogue, facts, own-context and paired photos require explicit final-base replay binding. The next neural hypothesis is task-specific trainable residual capacity over the own general backbone, with total unique parameters below one billion. Explicit code routing would allow complete problem-to-program supervision without updating the general route's base weights. This is proposed, not implemented, memory-qualified, trained or shown to improve anything. A concrete prototype must inspect actual interfaces, establish initial route equivalence and unchanged base state/outputs after real updates, use training-only whole original programmes with no held answers/clipping, respect shared-device/optimizer/storage limits, and pass matched code/stress plus retained-capability checks before any promotion. Existing four-GPU curriculum and all original remaining multimodal/RLHF stages remain intact.

The own code-residual prototype is now implemented and has completed an actual GPU mechanics check (`reports/code-residual-branch-v1-readiness.json`). It shares one original frozen 706,012,672-parameter general/vision/critic backbone and adds 150,994,944 trainable code parameters, for **857,007,616 total unique parameters** across explicit general/code routes. This builds on established [adapter-based transfer learning](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/houlsby19a.html); this residual placement/capacity is an unproven coding hypothesis, not a novel-method or capability claim. No second backbone or external model weights are used.

Eleven focused CPU checks pass, covering actual Adam updates, exact frozen-route equivalence and base-state invariance, text/image/padding, cached generation, versioned cache rejection, checkpointed gradients, adapter-only serialization and the total parameter cap. Two initial failed numerical comparisons remain in their original logs. Freezing or changing gradient mode can alter tiny CPU image-kernel rounding bits; the exact equivalence reference now uses the same frozen execution mode, while separate pre/post-freeze and gradient-mode comparisons keep explicit floating tolerances and unchanged argmax. This qualification is preserved rather than treating a changed numerical reference as a capability gain.

The observed loss/generation mismatch motivates research, not a proven causal diagnosis. [Welleck et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.04319) study objectives that penalize repetitive generations; [Holtzman et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.09751) study degeneration under decoding; [Finlayson et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01693) analyze truncation and the softmax bottleneck. None establishes that an objective or sampler change will fix this code model, solve algorithms, or meet frontier capability. A possible next controlled trial would compare a generation-aware objective with matched CE updates while protecting legitimate code repetitions and keeping the original greedy functional score unchanged. More diverse original admitted supervision remains another hypothesis. These objective or sampler changes are not implemented. Future trials must retain source roles, storage checks and promotion requirements. SmolLM's [primary pretraining notes](https://github.com/huggingface/smollm/blob/main/text/pretraining/README.md) also describe separate data/masking/stability ablations; their results do not transfer automatically to this sub-1B experiment. All final single-backbone source/replay integration, broader knowledge, image reading, retention, RLHF and independent high-end comparisons remain unmet.

2026-10-16: own learned conditional-code initialization under1B, without rewriting general weights.

The full-code policy only passes3/175supported public-Python problems and damages general generated answers5to1/559. The original own joint is a different weak but broader policy; freezing it while acquiring code-specific parameters addresses interference, without establishing high-end capability. [LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.09685v2) motivates shared frozen weights plus low-rank updates. [SVD-LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07378v5) emphasizes that generation preservation needs stronger treatment than a raw matrix norm. This experiment uses plain own-policy weight differences, not that paper's activation whitening/sequential recovery, and no external model/teacher or downloaded model weights.

OwnCodeDelta has a completely unchanged general TinyMultimodalLM and a second decoder view sharing its original matrix objects, vision encoder and critic. Code-view Q/K/V/O and three FFN matrices add rank352factors approximating own full-code minus own joint weights. Its embedding/head are tied to one separate own full-code embedding; code norms use the own full-code norms. The code view registers the shared original general view so every unique general/code parameter remains visible to inference evaluators. There is no mutable global route flag; checkpointed gradients and cached decoding use explicit separate views. Cache signatures reject wrong-route, changed-weight/dtype/device caches. Both routes remain own scratch architecture operations. The original general object is not modified, so code training cannot update general or point-query parameters; compatibility with a later qualified own point head is reserved, not yet a trained integrated model.

The changed dense policy now has its own-response actor/critic PPO kernel. It samples the unmodified full vocabulary at temperature 1, retains every whole original prompt, uses the counted frozen fused base for reference log probabilities, adds a human reward at the finite-horizon response endpoint, and normalizes advantages jointly over actual response tokens.
